  ### 5. Contract freeze before 1.0
  
  Pin these:
  1. **Exit codes.** 0/1/2 are specified. Define behaviour for interrupts and timeouts.
  2. **Error-code stability policy.**
     - Never reuse a number.
     - Add a duplicate and overload cleanup.
     - Decide that E051 and E170 stay stable or get split.
     - Declare whether diagnostic kebab codes are stable.
  3. **JSON schemas.**
     - `schema` publishes 8 types. Missing are `add`, `pre`, intent, artifact manifest, decision, release-pr documents and the diagnostics object.
     - Give `schemaVersion` one meaning, or document the per-document meaning.
     - Fix the `command` value on errors, the `escalatedBy` semantics, and `edgeKind` spelling (`Runtime` vs `peer`).
     - Note that `configPath` and `files` in init JSON are absolute paths.
  4. **Text output.** Declare it unstable except for the few strings that are quoted ("Nothing to release.", `[DRY-RUN]`, `Added changeset:`, the stderr warning formats).
  5. **Tag format.**
     - New default tags change namespace when a same-named sibling is added (ADR 11).
     - The tag message is fixed as `Release <tag>`, and the release workflow filename is fixed to `callisto-release.yml`.
     - Decide whether promotion is a 1.0 compatibility break.
  6. **Config schema.**
     - `deny_unknown_fields` makes every new key a break for older binaries.
     - Planned breaks are queued: requiring `registries.kind`, rejecting bare and colon-form references (ADR 12, proposed), and `nuget` accepted but not working. Decide these before 1.0.
  7. **Action inputs and outputs.** Spec them, including the two deprecated inputs that are ignored.
  8. **Wire versions.** wire-compat.md records current behaviour only, so there is no policy. The receipt stays at version 2 while its envelope is at 3.
  
  ### 6. Spec vs docs vs ADRs: contradictions found
  
  - **releasing.md vs spec** on retry limit and napi roster (section 1).
  - **architecture/release.md vs spec** on the npm and Cargo observation argv.
  - **ADR 1 vs spec and code** on four fixed gaps.
  - **Docs vs code** on `default_for`.
  - **ADR 9 and ADR 10** are stale in status only.
  - **No contradiction found between publishing.md and the spec** on credentials and PyPI sealing.
  - **ADR 3 vs REL-PUB-09 post-publish re-observation:** compatible. It is a per-operation confirm, not a post-pass.
  
  ### Not verified
  
  - **No real registry or GitHub run.** I did not run the publish, tag, release, attestation, rerun or PyPI observation paths (REL-PUB-02 to 09, REL-GH-*, REL-RERUN-*).
  - **`release execute` and `release artifact-manifest` with slots.** Not run, since they would publish.
  - **Action scripts and generated workflows.** Not run. I read only the REL-PR-04 retry loop (9 × 3 s).
  - **Dry-run digest equality** with a later receipt (REL-CMD-04).
  - **Interactive wizard and TTY behaviour.**
  - **E121 and E314 corner cases.**
  - **Python builds and sealed files.**
  - **Whether `macos-13` is retired.**

  | 14 | `crates/callisto-model/src/release/package_id.rs:~62` (`ReleasePackageId::parse`, strict `eco/name`) | Release decision file and on-disk wire. Test at line 275 asserts `cargo:shared` is rejected. | No | No |
  | 15 | `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/init.rs:118-127` (`product_package`: `package.id == name` string compare, else a bare-name compare) | `callisto init --product-package` (`cli.rs:231`) | No. `cargo:app` matches neither branch and gives `InitInvalidProductPackage`. | No |
  
  `resolve_human` (`identity.rs:358`) also parses colon, but it has no non-test callers. The only references are the unit tests at `identity.rs:20,32` and `walk/tests.rs:800`.
  
  There is no `status` or `version` CLI selector flag. `StatusArgs` has only `--strict` and `--check`. The "status/version selector" in the tests is a changeset entry, so surfaces #3 and #4.

  ## 3. Docs, spec and dogfood files showing the colon form
  
  **No colon form (grep-verified):** root `callisto.toml` (all slash: `product-package = "cargo/callisto-cli"`, three `[[release.artifact]]` entries, `[[package]] match = "cargo/callisto-cli"`; the fixed group uses bare names), `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/config.md`, `docs/releasing.md`, `docs/errors.md`, `.github/**`, `.changeset/*.md`, `crates/callisto-cli/tests/cmd/help.trycmd` (the only trycmd), and all `*.snap` files (init workflow, release derive, pr_body).
  
  **Colon form appears:**
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:23`, "`<ecosystem>/<name>` and `<ecosystem>:<name>` select the package whether or not it is promoted"
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:30`, `match = "<name>" | "<ecosystem>:<name>" | "<ecosystem>/<name>"`
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:38-39`, package-set glob, colon and slash both scope
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:128`, the group member criterion "`cargo:foo` or `cargo/foo` resolves…"
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:138`, the diagnostic lists candidates "(`cargo:foo`)". This is an output claim, see section 4.
  - `docs/specs/cli.json:72`, `callisto release [--package <ECOSYSTEM/NAME>|<ECOSYSTEM:NAME>]...`
  - `docs/architecture/identity.md:16` (table example `cargo:foo`), `identity.md:30` (`cargo:foo` and `npm:foo` as separate packages), `identity.md:18` (parse prefix text)
  - `crates/callisto-graph/CHANGELOG.md:287,351`. These are historical entries; the first says "qualify it with `npm:name`".
  - Source comments or messages:
    - `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:199` (error detail text)
    - `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:707`
    - `crates/callisto-graph/src/config/pattern.rs:10,17,25,52,53`
    - `crates/callisto-graph/src/config/resolve.rs:306`
  
  **Docs that already say colon is rejected, the target state:**
  - `docs/adr/0012-canonical-distribution-release-ownership.md:13` ("Reject the colon form everywhere")
  - `docs/architecture/polyglot-v1.md:18`
  - `docs/projects/V1-IMPLEMENTATION.md:23`
  - `docs/projects/V1-SYSTEM-HARDENING.json:45` (new code E316 "colon rejected with a code that names the slash form", E317 for a bare config reference; "Changesets and pre.json keep native names resolved at read time, with a hard error for colon")
  - `docs/projects/ROAD-TO-V1.md:48`
  
  E316 and E317 are not yet in `docs/errors.md`. Per those docs, a hard colon error is required for changesets and `pre.json` as well, even though bare native names stay allowed there.
  
  ## 4. Output that renders a colon-form id
  
  Everything that renders a `PackageId` goes through `display_name()` and so gives the slash form:
  - JSON via `Serialize` (identity.rs:232)
  - `Display`
  - tags (`tag.rs:74`)
  - matrix keys (`graph/src/matrix.rs:498,504,536`; `model/src/matrix.rs:12` doc)
  - CLI tables (`cli/src/render/mod.rs:42,77,96,113`)
  - errors (`graph/src/error.rs:77,91,98,117,128,135,151,168`)
  - the PR body
  - `pre_json_key` (`lib.rs:293`)
  - the changeset entry written by `add` (`add.rs:40`)
  - `ReleasePackageId` `Display` (`release/package_id.rs:84`, slash)
  
  **One colon emitter:** `crates/callisto-graph/src/identity.rs:483-487`, in `IdentityIndex::resolve_native_with_fallback`. It does `format!("{}:{n}", e.prefix())` and builds the `UnknownPackage` warning "dependency name `x` is ambiguous across ecosystems: cargo:foo, pypi:foo". It appears in the `status`, `version` and `matrix` diagnostics lists. The name is a dependency name from a manifest, not a user selector, and a nearby comment explicitly says no prefix advice is given. A user might still copy `cargo:foo` back as a selector. It is pinned by these assertions:
  - `identity.rs:76-77`, `resolve_native_with_fallback_returns_none_and_one_diagnostic_for_two_candidates`
  - `identity.rs:187,192`, `resolve_native_with_fallback_lists_the_candidates_own_native_name_not_the_primary_name`
  - `walk/tests.rs:1216,1245`, `npm_consumer_depending_on_cargo_only_name_gets_no_edge_and_diagnostic` and `npm_consumer_depending_on_serde_with_cargo_serde_present_gets_no_edge_and_diagnostic`
  - `crates/callisto-graph/tests/ecosystem_bugs_test.rs:308`, `cross_ecosystem_npm_to_cargo_dep_edge_is_discovered` (`cargo:rust-addon`)
  - `docs/specs/workspace.json:138`
  
  **Echoes of user input:** a group member written with colon is echoed as typed in `MissingGroupMember` (`walk/tests.rs:82` asserts `"npm:foo"`). The `package-set` "matched nothing" diagnostic (`walk.rs` near 789-798 and the `package_set_ecosystem_test.rs:84,130` assertions) echoes the user's raw colon pattern. Its advice text already says "use an ecosystem-prefixed pattern like `{eco}/{name}`" (slash).
  
  I did not find a status, version `--dry-run`, matrix or release-plan JSON field that prints a colon-form package id.

---

Found something really concerning about the NPM package release:

1. The release workflow can't finish a multi-package npm publish in one pass. After each npm publish, Callisto checks that the registry lists the new version. It waits only about 14 seconds (4 checks at 2, 4 and 8 seconds), and npm often takes minutes. When the check times out, Callisto stops with E157, even though the publish succeeded. Its documented recovery is "Re-run failed jobs", which picks up where it left off, one package per re-run. The same thing happened on 0.3.1, and I got it through then with repeated manual re-runs. With seven npm packages this time, the release was always going to stop and restart several times.

We need to dig much further into NPM package releases, how long it takes and what do we do here? NPM is not as strict about dependency packages existing before publish right so we could just fire away and check at the end, re-doing anything that had issues? We also should consider longer timeouts and dependent on the reason too right?

Think very thoroughly through this. Timeout might need to be configurable but a sane default needs to work

---

Also some feedback from codex:



<pasted_content id="b815">
# Adversarial review: October 14–21

Recommendation: merge #293 (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/pull/293) to restore release verification, then regenerate #248 (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/pull/248) before merging it. Hold #290 (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/pull/290) and its dependent PRs until the release-source guard covers the cases below.

I reviewed all six open PRs, the current origin/main release and identity paths, uncommitted files across the worktrees, the architecture and ADRs, and the latest release workflow failure. I reproduced two Git-history edge cases in isolated repositories. This is a prioritized review of reachable risks; I did not execute a live registry publication or inspect every line of the week’s merged commits.

## Findings

| Priority | Finding | Evidence and consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Release blocker | The green version PR is stale. | #248 (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/pull/248) consumes 15 changesets and its 0.12.0 changelog describes those changes. Current main has 32 changesets, including report and CLI breaking changes absent from that changelog. Merging the PR as it stands would ship the newer code without describing it in 0.12.0 and leave 17 changesets to trigger a later release for code already shipped. Its 12 checks pass, but the latest release workflow (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/actions/runs/39696020661) failed in tests because cargo-http-registry was missing; the PR refresh job was skipped. #293 supplies that tool and currently has passing checks. |
| High | #290 does not reliably bind a published version to its source commit. | Its version lookup (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/blob/d08baad248b7dd714cc01aa2b5687e808bdb6a73/crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release/version_commit.rs#L23) reads only the first canonical manifest’s literal version. A Cargo member using version.workspace = true yields no version commit, so a later source edit can still be tagged after the registry version is adopted. A manifest move makes the move commit appear to have set the version; my Git reproduction found zero “newer” commits even when the move changed source bytes. A change made only in a merge resolution is also missed because the shared Git history query excludes merge commits; my reproduction showed a changed package tree and zero counted commits. These are reachable forms of the mismatch #290 intends to stop. |
| High | #290 turns an uncertain precheck into permission to continue. |  published_packages (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/blob/d08baad248b7dd714cc01aa2b5687e808bdb6a73/crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release/capability.rs#L345) returns only a set and drops provider errors and indeterminate observations. If that call fails transiently, the guard proceeds. The executor’s later preflight (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/blob/d08baad248b7dd714cc01aa2b5687e808bdb6a73/crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release_execution.rs#L38) can then observe the version as exact, adopt it, and continue to tag the later commit. Propagating the error helps, but the source check must also be tied to the executor’s authoritative observation so two reads cannot disagree safely. |
| Medium | The new E176 message still loses operation identity. | #291’s Display implementation (https://github.com/orin-dx/callisto/blob/7d215456d28ce7063335e9e484e5fc632482da35/crates/callisto-model/src/release/operation.rs#L316) omits the registry key, artifact slot, and platform name. Two uploads for the same package and version therefore print the same operation in an indeterminate-observation error, although the durable operation IDs differ. Include the safe identifying field for each role and test two otherwise identical operations. |
| Local work, high if unintended | Entire is now configured to sync session records to a public remote. | The uncommitted .entire/settings.json (.entire/settings.json:9) changes push_sessions from false to true and enables checkpoint pushes. entire status identifies origin as the sync destination, and the GitHub repository is public. Entire’s storage documentation (https://github.com/entireio/cli/blob/main/docs/architecture/sessions-and-checkpoints.md) says pushed checkpoints contain metadata, prompts, and redacted transcripts. I found no evidence that this change has already uploaded this session. Decide explicitly whether public session provenance is intended before the next push; otherwise keep syncing off or use an approved private destination. |
| Local test debt | The untracked selector test cannot serve as a current regression gate. | The test file (.claude/worktrees/wf_cd627354-1a0-2/crates/callisto-cli/tests/status_version_selector_tests.rs:180) sits on a branch older than current main. It expects colon selectors and bare report IDs, which current main rejects or emits differently, and six discrepancy tests are ignored. Rebase it, update the current contract, and turn remaining real discrepancies into active tests or explicitly tracked gaps before adding it to CI. |

## The recurring issue

Several fixes this week correctly stopped treating failure as absence. #290 reintroduces that pattern in its ignored provider result, ignored historical parse result, and unwrap_or(None) on a parent-version read. Search sibling release and history paths for the same conversion and require each to distinguish absent, known exact, and unknown.

A second pattern is using a projection of authority as authority itself. A manifest path and version string stand in for the release source; a shortened display string stands in for an operation ID; green checks on an old PR stand in for a current release decision. The release model already has exact source and operation identities. The local route needs a check that preserves those identities through observation and execution, with a defined outcome when source provenance cannot be established.

The history guard also has a performance trap: it obtains the entire manifest commit list and only then takes 100 entries. That limit neither bounds the Git walk nor makes an older version commit safe to ignore. Resolve provenance with a bounded, explicit unknown outcome, or remove the silent limit; measure the Git calls on a large workspace.

## Recommended order

1. Merge #293, confirm the release workflow passes on main, and regenerate #248. Check that its decision, changelog, consumed changesets, and checks reflect current main before release.
2. Rework #290’s local release guard around effective historical versions and an explicit source-provenance result. Test inherited Cargo versions, a moved manifest, a merge-only package edit, an observation that fails then succeeds, and history beyond the current limit. Keep the final safety decision at the executor boundary.
3. Fix #291’s diagnostic identity. Keep #292 stacked only if #290 is corrected promptly; otherwise restack the dependency update independently. #289 duplicates #292’s dependency bump.
4. Rebase the untracked test and review the Entire sync policy. entire status --detailed also reports Claude Code and OpenCode hooks out of date, so adding one Claude hook has not cleared the installed CLI’s drift check.

I checked two apparent issues and did not count them as findings: GitHub documents $/ as valid same-repository action syntax, and the installed Entire CLI says Gemini support was retired, so removal of the Gemini hooks is intentional. No source files or existing worktree changes were edited during this review.
</pasted_content id="b815">

<pasted_content id="b815">
Yes, mostly. Callisto has three problems that turned this into hours of stalling:

1. It hides the real error. pnpm prints its errors to stdout, and Callisto's E163 message only includes stderr, so both failures show npm publish failed (exit 1): with nothing after it.
2. It has no time limit on the publish command. pnpm publish hung for 12 minutes on attempt 9 and 14.5 minutes on attempt 10 before exiting with an error.
3. It only waits about 14 seconds for npm to show a published package. That's why every platform package cost a failed run and a re-run (E157).

What's still unclear is why the main package's publish hangs at all. It's not a pnpm upgrade: both releases used pnpm 11.28.7, and the 0.3.1 publish of the same package went through in about 35 seconds. The hang looks like pnpm waiting for something that can't arrive in CI, such as a 2FA code or a browser login, but I can't confirm that without pnpm's output.

To see the error and likely finish the release, I can push a throwaway branch with a one-off workflow. It would run timeout 180 pnpm publish --filter=@orin-dx/michi --no-git-checks with the org npm token and print everything pnpm says. If the publish works, 0.4.0 is on npm. I'd then re-run the release once, and Callisto would skip the already-published package and create the michi-node@0.4.0 tag and the GitHub release. If it fails, we see the actual reason. Either way I delete the branch afterwards.

For Callisto, I'd write up the three bugs above as a fix brief for your Callisto session.
</pasted_content id="b815">

---

Release reconciliation fails on npm propagation lag

caliper v0.3.0 (orin-dx/caliper run 39808667033, job "Execute approved release"):
each attempt publishes one npm package, then the post-publish check runs ~35 s later,
the registry doesn't list the version yet, and the step exits with
"registry reported `<pkg>@0.3.0` published, but does not show it … registry propagation lag".
Re-runs skip already-published versions correctly, so the release finishes only by
re-running once per package (8+ npm packages → 8+ re-runs).

Expected: after a successful publish, poll for visibility with backoff (npm can take
minutes for new versions with provenance), or continue publishing the remaining packages
and reconcile visibility once at the end.

michi-graph 0.5.0 is published under the MIT licence and has no dependencies, so callisto-model can depend on it.
	•	It covers what Callisto's brief asked for: one validation call that reports every problem at once, with each cycle shown as a concrete loop. Edges can have kinds, filtered per validation. Ties break by key or by declaration order, deterministically. It also has ready(), layers(), ancestors and descendants, and no recursion.
	•	michi-pipeline 0.5.0 now validates and orders plans with michi-graph (pinned =0.5.0). It also includes these items from the brief's pipeline section:
	•	Precheck (Replay::Prechecked, Step::precheck returning Applied / NotApplied / Unknown). This is Callisto's observe-then-act model: a step whose effect already landed ends CompletedEarlier without running.
	•	Effects: a failure whose effect is unknown is never replayed blindly, which matches Callisto's "never retry an effect blindly".
	•	Durable starts: a step runs only after its start has been recorded. This was extra, not in the brief, and is close to Callisto's "Attempting only after a proof".

Not built yet, because the brief said to wait for Callisto's trial run to show they're needed:

	•	a poll step bounded by a deadline instead of a retry count;
	•	a shared deadline for a group of steps;
	•	a concurrency cap per key (for example, per registry);
	•	an adapter for blocking steps;
	•	typed outputs passed between steps.

For Callisto: it can add michi-graph = "0.5" to callisto-model now. A trial of michi-pipeline in its release path will show which of those five to build next.

The npm path passes no `--ignore-scripts`, so `prepublishOnly` and `prepack` scripts also see the npm token.

  Bug hunt of the manifests, graph, model and `add` code in `/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/.claude/worktrees/smoke-main`, read-only. No edits, builds or tests. `../callisto-findings/FINDINGS.md` does not exist, so nothing was skipped on account of it. I did not re-report the already-known items (A3, A4, A6, A9, A12 and the listed boundaries). 12 findings, ranked.
  
  **1. HIGH, CONFIRMED: Cargo and npm single-clause `>` and `<` ranges are rewritten to a range that excludes the new version, with no error.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs:853-859` (single clause) and `:820-826` (compound `>` branch); `crates/callisto-manifests/src/npm.rs:596-608`. Neither is checked afterwards in `crates/callisto-graph/src/cascade.rs:605-617`.
  - Code (Cargo):
    ```
    let (prefix, rest) = crate::common::split_single_operator_prefix(original)?;
    if rest.contains('*') { return None; }
    let rendered = format!("{prefix}{}", render_at_precision(target, rest));
    ```
    npm is the same shape.
  - Failing input:
    - Dependent `app` has `core = "<2.0.0"` (npm: `"core": "<2.0.0"`). `core` gets a major bump to `2.0.0`.
    - Coverage is `DoesNotCover`, so it rewrites to `<2.0.0`. That is the same text, and it excludes 2.0.0.
    - Result: `Rewritten`, no E319, and the dependent publishes a range its own dependency cannot satisfy.
    - `>1.0.0` with target 2.0.0 is already covered. The co-release floor raise in finding 2 turns it into `>2.0.0`, which also excludes 2.0.0. The same applies to the compound branch (`>1.0, <3` becomes `>2.0.0, <3`).
  - Contrast: the compound `<` branch (cargo.rs:827-844) checks the bound, but the single-clause path and the `>` prefix do not.
  - Fix: unify the Cargo and npm rewrite into one function, and assert `coverage(rewritten, target) == Covers` before returning `Some`.
  
  **2. HIGH, CONFIRMED: the floor-raise pass fails the whole run (E319) on a range that already covers the new version.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-graph/src/cascade.rs:439-484`, via `rewrite_edge` at `:605-617`. `docs/errors.md:315` defines E319 as "out of range ... cannot be rewritten".
  - Code: the pass skips only `Workspace`, `Catalog`, `Opaque` and covering `WorkspaceProtocol` specs, then calls `rewrite_edge(...)?`. A `LeftAlone` result for a non-dev edge becomes `Err(UnrewritableRange)`.
  - Failing inputs, when both packages release:
    - Python: a bare dependency `dependencies = ["lib-a"]`, the usual uv-workspace pattern. It parses to `Range(.., "*")`, and `python::round_trip` returns `None` (python.rs:767).
    - Cargo: `"*"`, `"1.*"`, or `">=1.2, <2"` (the `<2` fails `Version::parse`, cargo.rs:834).
    - npm: `"*"`, `"1.x"`, or an `||` set.
    - Python: `foo>=1,<3,!=2.1` or `foo<3`.
  - Outcome: the release run stops with "cannot be rewritten to cover X", although the range already covers X. The suggested fix ("edit the range") makes no sense for these.
  - The same pass also raises the floor of `Peer` and `Optional` edges on every co-release (`^1.0.0` becomes `^1.4.0`). That is probably unintended for peers.
  - Fix: in the floor pass, treat `LeftAlone` as a warning when coverage is `Covers`.
  
  **3. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: `git log` and `git show` calls lack `--no-show-signature`, so `log.showSignature=true` corrupts parsing.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs:530-567` (`commits_since`), `:593-607` (`paths_changed_since`), `:450` (`commit_adding`). Only `commit_time` (`:379`) passes the flag. Nothing in `callisto-cli/src/runner.rs` neutralizes git config.
  - Failing input: a developer or CI has `log.showSignature=true` or `--show-signature` aliased, and the repo has signed commits.
    - gpg text is emitted before the formatted record. `commits_since` then hits the "expected a `<sha>\x1f<message>` record" error (E301) on the leading chunk, or appends gpg lines to the previous commit's message.
    - In `paths_changed_since` (`-z --name-only`), the gpg lines glue onto the first path of each signed commit, so that path is lost. A package can be reported unchanged.
    - `commit_adding` returns gpg text plus the sha, which is not a valid sha.
  - Related: the doc comment claims `\x1e` makes records unambiguous (access.rs:11), but a commit message containing U+001E splits a record and fails with E301.
  - Related: pathspecs are passed unmagicked (`p.display()`, :545), so a package directory containing `[` or `*` is treated as a glob.
  
  **4. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: `[[package-set]] pre-major-inference` is parsed but never applied, and a `[[package]]` rule shadows a matching `[[package-set]]` wholesale.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:236-239`; `crates/callisto-graph/src/walk.rs:627-636`.
  - Code: `resolve_package_config(&pkg.id, &package_ecosystems, config).and_then(|pcfg| pcfg.pre_major_inference)`. `resolve_package_config` scans only `cfg.packages`. Elsewhere `package_sets` is read only in walk.rs.
  - Failing input:
    - `[[package-set]] match="cargo/*" pre-major-inference="conservative"` is silently inert. Provenance is recorded, so `config` output reports it as explicit.
    - `[[package-set]] release-trigger="auto"` plus `[[package]] cargo/foo {tag-template=...}` gives `foo` the default `changeset` trigger. `let set_override = if pkg_override.is_none() {..}` does no per-field merge.
  - Fix: one resolver that merges field by field, used by both aggregate and walk.
  
  **5. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: `callisto add` can write a changeset that the loader then rejects, which blocks `version` and `status`.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/add.rs:25-45`; `crates/callisto-model/src/format/changeset/mod.rs:203-235`.
  - Code: `write_changeset` checks only empty names, names containing a quote, and an empty summary. It has no duplicate check, while `parse_changeset` rejects duplicates (E047, mod.rs:166).
  - Failing input: `callisto add --package foo:minor --package cargo/foo:patch --summary x`. Both specs resolve to `display_name()` `foo`, so the written file has two `foo` lines. The next `version` or `status` hard-fails with E047 until the file is hand-edited. `load_changesets` (aggregate.rs:131) fails on the first bad file.
  - Fix: dedupe, or reject, on resolved id in `add`.
  
  **6. MEDIUM, PLAUSIBLE: npm ranges with space-separated comparators, hyphen ranges or a `v` prefix become `Opaque`, so nothing happens when the dependency bumps.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-model/src/version.rs:216-243` (npm ranges are parsed with the Rust `semver` crate); `crates/callisto-manifests/src/npm.rs:569-573`; `crates/callisto-graph/src/cascade.rs:245`.
  - Code: `semver::VersionReq::parse(&normalized)` per `||` arm. If I recall correctly, the Rust crate requires commas between comparators. `>=1.0.0 <2.0.0` then fails and the spec falls through to `DepSpec::Opaque`.
  - Failing input: `"core": ">=1.0.0 <2.0.0"`, then a major bump of `core`. Opaque gives `Coverage::Unknown`, which `cascade_action` treats as `Covers`. There is no bump, no rewrite, and no diagnostic (`!matches!(edge.spec, DepSpec::Opaque(_))` suppresses it).
  - Contradiction: `docs/errors.md:315` says spaced npm ranges reach E319, but they never reach rewrite.
  - Not fully traced because I did not run the crate; confirm with a unit test.
  
  **7. MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: Python rewrites lose precision, which narrows the range. npm and Cargo preserve precision; Python does not.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-manifests/src/python.rs:750-765` and `:772-779`; `render_target` at `:798-807`; `:783-791`.
  - Code: `render_target` always emits `{major}.{minor}.{patch}`, and `==` is rewritten whenever the spec `starts_with("==")`.
  - Failing inputs:
    - `==1.*` (a whole major) with target 2.0.0 becomes `==2.0.0`, an exact pin.
    - `~=1.4` (>=1.4,<2) with target 2.0.0 becomes `~=2.0.0` (>=2.0.0,<2.1). The next 2.1.0 release no longer fits.
    - Poetry `~1` becomes `~2.0.0` (>=2.0.0,<2.1.0).
  
  **8. MEDIUM-LOW, CONFIRMED: Poetry dependency rewrites drop the trailing comment and key decor on string-form dependencies.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-manifests/src/python.rs:597-605`. Cargo's `set_dependency_version` (cargo.rs:311-342) preserves decor.
  - Code: `table.insert(name, value(new_spec_str));` (and `fields.insert("version", value(..))`). `insert` replaces the whole key-value, so `foo = "^1.2"  # pinned for X` loses its comment.
  - Related: the `Table` branch of cargo.rs:332 restores only the value decor.
  
  **9. LOW-MEDIUM, CONFIRMED: an empty changeset (`--empty` shape) is never consumed.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:310-322` and `:413`.
  - Code: `if matched_any && !has_unresolved { consumed.push(..) }`. A changeset with zero entries leaves `matched_any` false.
  - Failing input: the file is accepted by the parser (mod.rs:179), but stays on disk forever. `version` keeps warning "No pending changesets", and `check_partial_run` (version.rs:416-426) counts any `.md` file as a pending release input.
  
  **10. LOW-MEDIUM, PLAUSIBLE: a fixed group aligns an untagged member that is ahead of the group, which downgrades it.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-graph/src/groups.rs:132-169`; `crates/callisto-graph/src/cascade.rs:424-435`.
  - Code: with released members present, the anchor is `released[0]` and `fixed_group_target` bumps only from that base. Every live member, including untagged ones, gets `winner`. Only released members are checked for divergence (groups.rs:40-65).
  - Failing input: group {A tagged at 1.0.0, B untagged on disk at 2.0.0}, with a patch changeset on A. Both targets are 1.0.1, so B goes from 2.0.0 to 1.0.1. `write_version_guarded(from=2.0.0, to=1.0.1)` writes it, and no per-member regression check applies.
  
  **11. LOW, CONFIRMED: changelog `prepend` mishandles a BOM or a non-matching H1.**
  - Location: `crates/callisto-graph/src/changelog/write.rs:57-78`.
  - Failing inputs:
    - A `CHANGELOG.md` starting with a BOM does not match `# {name}\n`. The code falls to the "no matching H1" branch, emitting a new H1 above the old content with a U+FEFF embedded mid-file.
    - A file whose H1 is `# Changelog` (Keep-a-Changelog style) gets a second `# <display name>` H1 inserted above it on the first release.
  
  **12. LOW, mixed, plausible format-preservation gaps.**
  - `WorkspaceCargoResolver::persist` (cargo.rs:1074-1078) skips the BOM/CRLF fingerprint that `CargoToml::persist` applies (cargo.rs:401). `read_from` (:941-952) does not strip the BOM. A workspace-root `Cargo.toml` rewritten through the resolver can lose its BOM. I did not verify whether toml_edit drops it on parse.
  - npm `update_dependency_spec` (npm.rs:498-504) overwrites any string entry in `overrides`/`resolutions` that matches the dependency name. This replaces intentional pins and `$name` references with the dependent's range.
  - npm `persist` always re-serializes through serde_json's pretty printer. Inline arrays are expanded, `\uXXXX` escapes are normalized, and a minified `package.json` is expanded to 2-space.
  - `detect_fingerprint` stores the indent as `leading.len() as u8` (npm.rs:103, pre.rs:227), which truncates indents wider than 255. This is harmless in practice.
  
  **Checked, no bug found:** `atomic_write`'s fsync and parent-directory fsync, symlinked-package rejection, `parse_changeset` CRLF/BOM/quoted-name handling, semver and PEP 440 `checked_add` overflow in bumps, and toposort/cycle reporting. I found no HashMap iteration reaching output. Python `normalize_caret` and `normalize_wildcard` use unchecked `+ 1` on `u64` values parsed from the manifest (python.rs:245-293), which wraps silently in release builds. This is negligible.
  
  Key files, all under `/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/.claude/worktrees/smoke-main/crates/`:
  - `callisto-manifests/src/{cargo,npm,python}.rs`

  Ten findings, ranked by realistic impact on a repo with about 500 packages and 50k commits. Nothing in the repo was changed and no cargo ran. One measurement used the existing `target/debug/callisto` against a synthetic repo in `/tmp/claude-501/perf/r200`: 200 cargo packages, 200 annotated tags, about 500 commits. `callisto status` there took 14.6 s wall (sys 10 s). `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT` counted 604 git spawns: 200 `rev-parse --verify`, 200 `show`, 200 `log --no-merges`, 1 `tag --list`. Every other cost below is estimated from the code, assuming 5-30 ms per git spawn and 0.5-2 s per registry or network call.
  
  Housekeeping: I briefly wrote a scratch `.claude/scratch-perf/mk.sh` inside the worktree and removed it. A PATH-shadowing git wrapper was denied by the classifier and I did not retry it. The worktree has no changes left from me.
  
  **1. The executor re-verifies git trust with 7 spawns before every operation, including ones it only observes.**
  - Where: `commands/release_execution.rs:52` (`capability.recheck_trust()?;`, called before `preflight`), `commands/release/capability.rs:109`, `callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs:194-262`, `commands/release/binding.rs:200`.
  - Cost: O(V) × (7 spawns + one worktree scan). V is the number of operations, roughly 3-4 per package (publish, tag, forge release, forge publish, plus platform and upload ops), so about 1.5-2k at N=500.
  - Evidence: each `recheck_trust` runs `rev-parse --show-toplevel`, `--show-object-format`, `--is-shallow-repository`, `rev-parse --verify HEAD^{commit}`, `symbolic-ref`, `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all`, then `remote get-url --push origin` via `run_quiet`. That is about 12k spawns per run, and the `status` calls scan the tree each time.
  - A rerun where everything is already satisfied pays this for every operation and performs no effect. Estimate: several minutes to 10+ minutes of pure overhead.
  - Fix:
    - Move `recheck_trust()` into the `Proceed` branch, just before `Attempt`/`issue`/`publish`. Operations adopted as already satisfied cause no effect and don't need it.
    - Collapse the 4 `rev-parse` calls into one (`rev-parse --show-toplevel --show-object-format --is-shallow-repository HEAD`).
    - Take head, detached state and cleanliness from one `status --porcelain=v2 --branch`.
  - Trade-off: this changes when the stale-worktree guard fires, so confirm it with the owner.
  
  **2. The executor observes registries, tags and forge releases strictly one at a time.**
  - Where: `commands/release_execution.rs:45-86` (main loop) and `:192-240` (`settle_issued`, `for id in issued.clone()` at :207). `provider/registry.rs:55-60`, `:259-280`, `provider/tag.rs:139-170`, `provider/policy.rs:58-90`.
  - Cost: O(V × per-op latency), with no overlap between independent observations. Per package the serial cost is roughly:
    - registry check: `cargo info` or `npm view`, 0.7-2 s
    - tag check: `ls-remote` over the network, 0.3-1 s, plus the 50 ms poll floor from finding 7
    - forge: two `gh api` calls, 0.5-1 s each
    - Estimate: 3-5 s per package, about 25-40 minutes for a no-op rerun at N=500.
  - The doc comment on `settle_issued` says issued operations "are looked at together", but the loop is sequential. Each look pass over K issued npm publishes is K `npm view` spawns, about 8 minutes at K=500, and the wait window only counts sleeps, not look time.
  - Cargo and PyPI don't defer confirmation. `confirmed_registry_observation` sleeps 15 s / 45 s / 2 min / 3 min inside the serial loop (PyPI window 360 s, cargo 120 s), so any lag blocks the whole release.
  - When a version is adopted, the same registry is queried twice. `registry.rs:60` calls `warn_if_foreign_repository`, which runs a second `cargo info` or `npm view <spec> repository` after the preflight query already succeeded.
  - Fix:
    - Before the loop, run the read-only `preflight` for all operations on a bounded pool (8-16 threads via `std::thread::scope`; `Sleeper` is already `Sync`). Keep the effects serial.
    - Make the `settle_issued` pass concurrent the same way.
    - Parse `repository:` from the stdout of the existing `cargo info` call instead of calling again.
    - Fetch `git ls-remote origin 'refs/tags/*'` once instead of one call per tag.
  
  **3. `TagIndex::build` spawns git twice per package, and local release builds it twice.**
  - Where: `tags.rs:216-247` calls `history::may_have_held_name_at` for every package with a bare id (`history.rs:144` → `first_manifest_declares_at` → `GitAccess::read_at`, `access.rs:430`).
  - Cost: `read_at` is `rev-parse --verify <tag>:./path` followed by `show <tag>:./path`. That is 2 spawns per tagged bare-id package and one manifest parse at the tag.
  - Measured: 400 of the 604 spawns on 200 packages, which is 2N per build.
  - If the manifest path is absent at the tag, `Rosters::declaring_at` adds 3 whole-tree `git grep -i -F -l` calls per package, with no sharing across packages.
  - `callisto release` loads three workspaces: `load_workspace` (`cli/commands/release.rs:58`), `plan_local_release` (`local.rs:97`) and `validate_local_release_intent` (`capability.rs:231`). Two of them build the tag index, so about 4N spawns.
  - Smaller piece: `tags.rs:297` runs `select_last_tag` over all T tags per bare package, and `matching_tags` rescans all T per distinct template. That is O(N×T) string work, about 12M operations at 500 packages × 25k tags. It is only sub-second to a few seconds.
  - Fix:
    - Batch the manifest reads into one `git cat-file --batch` using the existing `batch_manifest_blobs_at` in `release_decision.rs`, and check only the chosen tag.
    - Skip the check when no other ecosystem in the graph could have held the name.
    - Index tags by their literal prefix (the text before the first `@`) instead of globbing every tag per package.
  
  **4. `changed_since_last_tag` runs one log walk and one worktree diff per distinct tag commit.**
  - Where: `changed.rs:30-110` (the `for (since, members) in groups` loop), `access.rs:593-640`.
  - Cost: with independent versioning, group count G ≈ N. Each group runs `git log --no-merges --full-history --name-only -z <tag>..HEAD` over the whole repo, not path-limited. That is O(range commits × files per commit) per group. For any package with no committed change, it also runs `git diff --name-only <tag> --`, which stats the whole worktree.
  - Then `touches` (`changed.rs:92`) is O(paths × specs) for every member. Total is O(N × P), where P is the number of paths changed since an old tag, up to hundreds of thousands. That is about 250M `starts_with` calls at N=500, P=500k.
  - Measured: 200 `log` spawns for 200 packages.
  - Fix:
    - Run one `git log --name-only` from the oldest tag commit, record the first commit touching each package path, and compare positions against `git rev-list`. This keeps the `--full-history` semantics.
    - Replace `git diff <tag>` against the worktree with tree-to-tree `git diff --name-only <tag> HEAD`, plus one shared `git diff --name-only HEAD`. This drops the per-tag worktree stat; it can differ from the old check when a worktree edit restores a file to its tag content.
    - Replace the `touches` scan with a sorted path list and a prefix binary search.
  
  **5. `release-pr decide` runs the full `status()` to read one boolean.**
  - Where: `callisto-cli/src/commands/release_pr.rs:121-124` (`let status = status(&workspace, ...)`; only `status.has_changesets` is used).
  - Cost: `status` builds the tag index (finding 3), runs `plan_version` (aggregate, per-package inference, cascade) and runs `changed_since_last_tag` (finding 4). That is O(N) spawns plus history walks on every base-branch run, and it is the measured 14.6 s at N=200, growing roughly linearly.
  - `has_changesets` is `packages.iter().any(|p| !p.pending_changesets.is_empty())` (`status.rs:181`). It depends only on `load_changesets_permissive` plus `resolve_pending_changesets`, which `status.rs:73-81` already calls first.
  - Fix: compute that boolean from those two calls and skip the rest.
  - Behavior change: `decide` would no longer fail on a cascade or tag error that `status` currently propagates.
  
  **6. `packages_moved_past_their_version` does an unbounded history walk and many spawns per selected package.**
  - Where: `commands/release/version_commit.rs:27-100`, called from `local.rs:154` on every local plan, preview included.
  - Cost per selected package:
    - `commit_setting_version` runs `commits_since(None, [manifest])`, which is `git log --no-merges --full-history --format=%H<FS>%B -- <manifest>` over the manifest's entire history with full message bodies. The result is truncated to `HISTORY_LIMIT = 100` only after git has produced all of it.
    - Each examined commit costs 2 spawns (`read_at`), plus 2 more for the parent.
    - A second path-limited `commits_since(version_commit, package_paths)` follows.
  - Without commit-graph Bloom filters, each log is O(total commits). That is about 1-3 s per package at 50k commits, so 100 selected packages cost 3-8 minutes and a full 500-package release 30+ minutes.
  - Fix:
    - Pass `-n HISTORY_LIMIT` and `--format=%H` to the log.
    - Use `git rev-list --count <v>..HEAD -- paths` for the count.
    - Read each version blob and its parent blob with one `cat-file --batch`.
  
  **7. Every `run_with_timeout` / `run_quiet` call has a 50 ms floor and spawns two reader threads.**
  - Where: `callisto-cli/src/runner.rs:488-519` (`child.try_wait()` … `std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50))`). Plain `run` uses `.output()` and has no such floor.
  - Cost: the first `try_wait` right after `spawn` almost always sees the child still running, so every call takes at least 50 ms. A 3 ms `rev-parse` therefore costs 50 ms.
  - Callers on the release path:
    - `prepared_git_remote`, run in `recheck_trust` for each operation: about 2000 × 50 ms ≈ 100 s.
    - The tag provider's `observed_local_tag`, 2 calls each, run on preflight, publish and post-push (`tag.rs:248-281`): about 0.3 s per tag, roughly 2.5 minutes at N=500.
    - `ls-remote`, `gh`, `cargo info`, `npm view`.
  - Fix: poll with a short backoff (1 ms doubling to 50 ms), or call `child.wait()` on a helper thread and use `recv_timeout`.
  
  **8. Per-package tag resolution and history walks in `aggregate`, and N `rev-parse` calls in `releases_tagged_at`.**
  - Where:
    - `aggregate.rs:171` and `:244`: `resolve_since` is one `rev-parse` per `release_trigger = auto` package, followed by `infer` → `commits_since`, one `git log --no-merges --full-history --format=...%B -- <pkg paths> <tag>..HEAD` per package (`access.rs:530`).
    - `release_decision.rs:285-310`: `git.resolve_commit(&tag)` (`:305`) for every package whose current version is tagged. In steady state that is every package, so N spawns on every `callisto release` and `release --dry-run`.
  - Cost: O(N) spawns plus N path-limited walks. A package that is untagged or has an old tag walks up to the whole history, about 500 × (1-5 s) in the worst case. Lockstep repos with short ranges are cheap.
  - Fix:
    - Build the tag-to-peeled-commit map once, replacing `git tag --list` in `fetch_all_tags` with `git for-each-ref refs/tags --format='%(refname:strip=2) %(*objectname) %(objectname)'`.
    - Use that map for `resolve_since`, `releases_tagged_at` and the `changed.rs` grouping.
    - For inference, do a single `git log --name-only` from the oldest tag and bucket commits per package.
  
  **9. `staged_changes_since` runs `git hash-object` once per changed file.**
  - Where: `callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs:130-133` and `:173-176`, used by `release-pr commit-plan` (`release_pr.rs:161`).
  - Cost: 2N+ spawns for a release PR. A version bump touches each package's manifest and changelog, plus lockfiles and changesets. Estimate: about 1000 spawns, 10-25 s at N=500.
  - Fix: verify all files with one `git hash-object --stdin-paths`, or hash `blob <len>\0<bytes>` in-process, since the bytes are already read. `new_sha` from the `diff --cached --raw` output is already available to compare against.
  
  **10. The executor recomputes the full eligible set and ancestor closure for every operation, then keeps only the first.**
  - Where: `release_execution.rs:45` (`eligible_in(&dag, &state, &issued).first().cloned()`) and `:260-278`.
  - Cost:
    - `eligible_in` returns a fully collected `Vec`. For each ready node (R of them) it runs `dag.ancestors()`, which allocates `vec![false; V]`, does a DFS and sorts.
    - It then does one `BTreeMap<ReleaseOperationId>` state lookup per ancestor. This is O(V × R × (V + A log A)) over the run.
    - With V≈2k, R in the hundreds (wide leaf layers) and A≈100, that is plausibly 5-15 s of CPU.
    - It is not dominant next to findings 1-2, but it is avoidable and quadratic or worse.
  - Fix: return an iterator from `eligible_in` and use `.next()` or `.find`. `dag.ready` is already lazy and in rank order, so the closure runs only for candidates until the first eligible one.
  
  Considered and left out:
  - The O(selected × deps × N) scan in `derive.rs:820-855`.
  - The O(E × N) canonicalize in `walk.rs:33-70`, which only applies to `workspace:../path` dependencies.
  - The cascade `.cloned()` edge copies.
  - Serial `gh attestation verify` per artifact slot, which only matters above about 50 slots.
  - Repeated `base_versions()` calls, which are served from the manifest cache.

Findings are ranked by severity, with the version-commit guard design proposal at the end.
  
  ## 1. The version-commit guard fails open on common layouts
  **Severity:** High. **Confidence:** High for 1a and 1b, medium for 1c to 1e.
  
  `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/release/version_commit.rs:25-26` documents it: "A package whose version commit cannot be found ... is not reported: the check only ever refuses on evidence." The cases:
  
  - **1a. Inherited versions (:93-99).** `version_at` keeps only literals:
    ```
    Some(VersionSource::Literal(version)) => Some(version),
    _ => None,
    ```
    With `version.workspace = true` (the common Cargo layout), `commit_setting_version` returns `Ok(None)` for every commit and the package is skipped via `continue` at :47-49. Scenario: a workspace-inherited crate's `cargo publish` lands, the run dies before the tag, a later commit touches the crate, and a rerun tags unpublished code at HEAD.
    - `read_effective_version`, which already resolves inheritance and returns `authority`, exists at `crates/callisto-manifests/src/lib.rs:66`, but the guard doesn't use it.
  - **1b. The 100-commit cap (:12-13, :69-72).** `.take(HISTORY_LIMIT)` runs after `commits_since(None, ...)` has already read the entire manifest history with full `%B` bodies. If more than 100 manifest-touching commits sit above the setter, for example a never-released `0.1.0` with steady dependency edits, it returns `None` and the guard is skipped. Hitting the cap is indistinguishable from "version never set".
  - **1c. Raw string versus rendered version (:46, :75).** The check is `version_at(..).as_deref() != Some(version)`, comparing the raw manifest literal to `entry.target_version.render()`. A literal that differs textually from the rendered version, such as a PyPI or semver spelling difference or build metadata, never matches (PLAUSIBLE).
  - **1d. Only the first manifest and first identity (:36-45).** `package.canonical_manifests().next()` and the first matching decision entry are used. For a dual-identity package, `among` in `local.rs:163` holds only that one identity, so `published_packages` checks only its registry publish. A dynamic `pyproject` version also yields `None` (PLAUSIBLE, low).
  - **1e. The path-level silent fallback (:79).** `parent = version_at(git, "{sha}^", ..).unwrap_or(None)` turns a real git error into "version was set here".
  - **Sibling sweep.** The guard exists only in `plan_workspace_release` (`local.rs:194`). `release plan --package` (`crates/callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:437`, via `derive_selected_release_decision`) has no equivalent (PLAUSIBLE).
  
  ## 2. The guard refuses a legitimate same-commit rerun in merge-commit workflows, and its remedy tags the wrong commit
  **Severity:** Medium-High. **Confidence:** High on code, not executed.
  
  `version_commit.rs:50` computes `git.commits_since(Some(&version_commit), ...)`. `crates/callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs:539` expands that to `format!("{r}..HEAD")` with `--no-merges` at :533. That is "reachable from HEAD and not from V", not "after V".
  
  Scenario:
  1. A release-PR branch is cut from main, and its commit V bumps the version.
  2. main takes commit M1, which touches `pkg/`.
  3. The PR is merged with a merge commit Mg, so M1 is not an ancestor of V.
  4. A run at Mg publishes the crate and dies before the tag.
  5. A rerun at Mg finds V as the setter. `V..Mg` contains M1, so `newer_commits >= 1`.
  6. The registry reads Exact, so `local.rs:165-171` returns `ReleasePublishedVersionMoved` (E320), although HEAD is exactly the published commit.
  7. The remedy in `docs/specs/release.json:299` ("checking it out and running `callisto release` again adopts the published version and tags that commit") then tags V, which lacks M1, not the published tree.
  
  The inverse also holds. `--no-merges` ignores edits that exist only inside a merge commit (conflict resolution), so `newer_commits` stays 0.
  
  ## 3. A local-only tag is accepted as proof the release finished
  **Severity:** Medium-High. **Confidence:** High.
  
  - In `provider/tag.rs:72-89`, `publish` runs `git.create_tag(...)` first and `push_tag(...)?` second.
  - A push failure (network or auth) leaves a local annotated tag at X, with no remote tag and no forge release.
  - `release_decision.rs:213` is `if version_is_tagged(tags, ..) && !ids.iter().any(|id| resumable.contains(id)) { continue; }`.
  - `TagIndex` is built from `git tag --list` (`access.rs:483-497`, called from `tags.rs:196`), so it sees local tags only.
  - `resumable` is non-empty only when the tag resolves to HEAD (`release_decision.rs:305`, `releases_tagged_at`).
  - Scenario: the push fails at X, the developer adds commit Y, and `callisto release` at Y says "Nothing to release" (`local.rs:183-186` returns `None`). The remote tag, forge draft, assets and publication never happen.
  - The same holds for any tag on an older commit with unfinished downstream operations. They are never observed, which `release.md` states as design. The local-only push-failure case is the part nobody gets told about.
  
  ## 4. `published_packages` turns every provider failure into "not published"
  **Severity:** Medium. **Confidence:** High.
  
  `capability.rs:417` is `matches!(observed, Ok(ProviderObservationV1::Exact { .. }))`. Indeterminate, Conflict, timeout and Err all mean "not published", and the guard passes.
  
  Scenario: the guard's `cargo info` or `npm view` fails after the observation retry budget is spent (5 attempts, `policy.rs:225-246`). The registry recovers before the executor's own preflight read. That read returns Exact, the version is adopted via `AlreadySatisfied`, and the tag lands on a later HEAD.
  
  - The doc comment at :392 ("the run reports it itself") assumes the run re-fails, which it doesn't when the failure was transient.
  - Contrast `unsatisfied_packages` (:362), where the same swallow fails safe, because an error counts as unsatisfied and the package stays in the plan.
  
  ## 5. Adoption through the publish path bypasses the guard and the foreign-repository warning
  **Severity:** Medium-Low. **Confidence:** Medium-High.
  
  - `RegistryProvider::preflight` calls `warn_if_foreign_repository` only on `AlreadySatisfied` (`registry.rs:59-61`).
  - If preflight reads Absent (stale or racing) and the publish client then answers "already exists", `run_publish_client` discards the outcome via `.map(|_outcome| ())` (:153-159). `confirm` or `settle` observes Exact, and the state becomes `Published`. No warning is printed and the guard never ran.
  - Scenario, concurrent runs: a local `callisto release` and a CI run (or two local checkouts) at different commits X and Y. Only CI is serialized, by the `release-execute` group. The run at Y publishes X's bytes under its own `Published`, then tags Y.
  - ADR 3 removed the workspace lock, and nothing replaced the guard's TOCTOU. The tag provider does fail closed for the second tagger (the remote tag conflicts).
  
  ## 6. Substring classification lets a failed publish count as "already published"
  **Severity:** Medium. **Confidence:** High on code, impact is rarer.
  
  `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/registry_argv.rs:495` has `if combined.contains("already exists") || combined.contains("already uploaded") { return Ok(AlreadyPublished) }` before the exit-code check. The npm check at :519-526 matches `epublishconflict`, `previously published`, `cannot publish over`, `e409` and `409 conflict`, again before `output.success()`.
  
  - `combined` is the whole stdout plus stderr, including `cargo publish`'s verification build log, which echoes source lines in compiler snippets, and npm's `shasum` and integrity lines.
  - A failing publish whose output contains one of these substrings returns `Ok(())` from `run_publish_client`.
  - **Cargo:** the executor then waits the 2-minute confirm window and ends in `RegistryPublishUnconfirmed`. The real cargo error is lost, other than what was streamed live.
  - **npm:** `issue` succeeds (`execution.rs:72-74`). The op is added to `issued`, and `eligible_in` releases dependent registry publishes (:269) while the dependency never landed. Those dependents publish pointing at a missing version.
  - The outcome is discarded anyway, so the classifier adds no information. It only converts a failure into "go observe".
  - The fix is in the design section.
  
  ## 7. Confirmation windows count sleeps only, not wall time
  **Severity:** Medium. **Confidence:** High.
  
  - `settle_issued` (`execution.rs:198-236`) advances `waited += wait` with sleeps only.
  - Each look calls `capability.settle` and then `observe`, i.e. `registry_observation` and `retry_observation` (up to 5 attempts, 2/4/8/16 s sleeps, `policy.rs:225-246`). Each attempt may run its full `timeouts::REGISTRY_QUERY` of 300 s (`policy.rs:20`).
  - A timeout becomes `CommandUnavailable`, then Transient (`registry.rs:507` and :708).
  - Result: with K issued npm packages and a blackholed registry, one look costs about K x (5 x 300 + 30) s, about 25 minutes per package. The advertised `confirm-timeout` (6 minutes) is not a wall-clock bound.
  - `retry_within_window` (`policy.rs:94-119`, used by the Cargo and PyPI confirm) has the same defect.
  - Fix: track an `Instant` deadline and pass the remaining time into the per-attempt timeout.
  
  ## 8. Foreign or squatted names are adopted with a warning at most
  **Severity:** Medium. **Confidence:** High on code. The doc declares it by design.
  
  - Adoption is `AlreadySatisfied`, and `repository.rs:35-50` only prints a warning.
  - A registry that records no repository gives no warning at all (`docs/architecture/release.md` line 78).
  - Scenario: someone else's `cargo/foo 0.1.0` exists. The first release of your own `foo 0.1.0` shows Exact. The run tags your commit and makes the forge release, and `Released N package(s)` succeeds.
  - The receipt records `AlreadySatisfied` and an exit code of 0, with no gate for CI.
  - Even a mismatch that is certain (registry names repo A, `origin` is B) only warns.
  - An opt-in `--adopt-foreign` or config flag, or refusing on a certain mismatch, would close it.
  
  ## 9. The local route can tag and push a commit that is on no remote branch
  **Severity:** Low-Medium. **Confidence:** Medium. `local.rs:122-126` allows `AnyHead`, and nothing checks that HEAD is an ancestor of a remote ref (grep for `merge-base`, `is_ancestor` and `@{u}` finds nothing in the release path). `git push <endpoint> <tag>` uploads the commit objects with the tag, so a release tag can point at an unmerged feature-branch commit. Spec and docs state "any branch", so this is a missing guard rather than drift.
  
  ## 10. The confirmation window restarts after each confirmed publish
  **Severity:** Low. **Confidence:** High.
  
  `execute_release` (`execution.rs:44-51`) calls `settle_issued` whenever nothing is eligible. Each call starts with `waited = 0` and `step = 0` (:203-204), and it returns on the first progress (:214). The doc claim (`release.md` line 70, "until each is shown or the longest `confirm-timeout` is spent") is not what the code does. The total wait for a publish that never appears can be several times the window, and the spacing schedule from the latest commit restarts at 15 s.
  
  ## 11. The credential-strip lists are fixed names
  **Severity:** Low. **Confidence:** Medium (depends on the operator's environment).
  
  - `runner.rs:52-63` and `registry.rs:390-401` are fixed lists. The redaction code treats `CARGO_REGISTRIES_*_TOKEN` and any `*_TOKEN`, `*_SECRET` or `*_PASSWORD` as secrets (`callisto-model/src/registry.rs` `known_credential_env_values`), but those are not withheld from children.
  - The `python -m build` backend, `curl`, `git` and `twine` still inherit them (for example a `CARGO_REGISTRIES_X_TOKEN` or a `MATURIN_PYPI_TOKEN` from the operator's shell).
  - This contradicts `release.md` line 52, "The build backend receives no registry or forge credentials".
  - One stripping rule, driven by the same predicate as redaction, would remove the mismatch.
  
  ## Smallest sound design for the four requested guard issues
  
  1. **Locate the version setter (inherited versions).**
     - Read versions through `callisto_manifests::read_effective_version`, with `manifest_at = |p| git.read_at(rev, p)`.
     - Compare parsed `Version` values, not raw strings.
     - Track both the member manifest and `EffectiveManifestVersion.authority` as pathspecs.
  
  2. **History cap and merge handling.**
     - Walk `git log --first-parent --format=%H -- <manifest> <authority>` with merges included.
     - Walk newest-first until `version_at(c) == V && version_at(c^1) != V`.
     - That C is the mainline commit where the version landed: a squash commit, or the merge commit itself in merge-style repos.
     - Drop the cap. The loop already stops at C, and its cost is proportional to the manifest-touching commits since the release.
     - Returning `None` after exhausting history or an unreadable commit means "unknown", never "fine".
  
  3. **Moved test.** Replace the commit count with the tree comparison `git diff --quiet C HEAD -- <package_paths>`. This catches edits made only in merge commits, ignores reverts and no-ops, and removes the `V..HEAD` reachability false positive from finding 2.
  
  4. **Fail closed on provider errors.**
     - Make `published_packages` return `Result`. Exact means published, Absent means not published, and anything else returns an error rather than "not published".
     - If C is unknown and the registry shows Exact, refuse with the instruction "run at the published commit, or tag it by hand".
     - Better still, enforce the guard at the moment of adoption. In the executor, when a registry op is adopted (`AlreadySatisfied`, or `Published` via AlreadyPublished) and its package's tag op is still `Pending`, require the tree-equality check against C before the tag may run.
     - That one check replaces both the plan-time guard and the second observation path, and it closes findings 4 and 5 (the TOCTOU and the unguarded adoption path).
     - For Cargo and npm there is an optional stronger signal: the published commit is recorded by the registry, in `.cargo_vcs_info.json` inside the `.crate` and in the `gitHead` field of `npm view`, where present. `git diff --quiet <that sha> HEAD -- paths` would then need no history search. I did not verify those fields exist for every publish path.
  
  Finding 6 has its own small fix. On a non-zero publish exit, run one confirmation observation. If it is Exact, treat the version as published. Otherwise return the tool's own error, with no substring matching.

  Read-only architecture and semantic-model review of /Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/.claude/worktrees/smoke-main. No edits, no cargo. Paths below are relative to `crates/` unless they start with `docs/`, `justfile` or `AGENTS.md`.

It started at 69be37933 (`feat/spaced-confirmation-looks`, clean); at 22:12 another session rewrote it to 5b59006ff with uncommitted edits in `callisto-graph/src/commands/release/version_commit.rs` and `callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs`.   ## Findings, ranked by cost of fixing after 1.0
  
  **1. Durable digests take their preimage from derived `Ord` variant order, `Debug` output and ad-hoc serde JSON**
  - `callisto-model/src/release/operation.rs:420` `prerequisites.sort();` and `callisto-model/src/release/intent.rs:164-168` hash prerequisites in that order; `ReleaseOperationRole` (`operation.rs:67`) derives `Ord`, so inserting a role variant anywhere but the end changes every existing intent digest (the enum already grew twice: `ForgePublish`, `PlatformPublish`). `canonical_operation_order` ("smallest topological order by operation id", docs/architecture/release.md:13) has the same dependency.
  - `callisto-model/src/release/decision.rs:184-195` hashes `serde_json::to_string(reason)` where `ReleaseInclusionReason` (`decision.rs:21-40`) carries `group_id: String`, `edge_kind: String`, `policy_id: String`. `callisto-graph/src/commands/release_decision.rs:1034` fills `edge_kind: format!("{dep_kind:?}")` (yields `"Peer"`) while `:1049` fills `edge_kind: "peer".to_string()` for `PeerEscalation`: one concept, two digest-visible spellings, and renaming a `DepKind` variant silently changes committed decision digests.
  - `callisto-model/src/release/artifact.rs:237` `ArtifactDigest::from_bytes(serde_json::to_vec(self)...)`: the only digest not on `CanonicalTranscript`; field order and `skip_serializing_if` are the preimage.
  - `callisto-model/src/release/digest.rs:175` `const VERSION: u8 = 1;` is shared by the decision, snapshot, intent and registry-binding transcripts (docs/architecture/wire-compat.md:39 admits it), so one domain's layout change bumps all four.
  - Fix: explicit `digest_tag()`/`digest_rank()` per enum used for sorting and hashing; `edge_kind: DepKind` with one camelCase serde spelling; `group_id: GroupName`; artifact digest on `CanonicalTranscript`; one version byte per transcript domain.
  
  **2. Wire-format versioning is inconsistent and `pre.json` has none**
  - `callisto-model/src/release/report.rs:17,37,60,81,103,123`: `command: "release"` stdout carries `schemaVersion` 2 (receipt), 1 (nothing-to-release) or 5 (dry-run intent) depending on payload; `callisto-cli/src/output.rs:21` overwrites the flattened artifact's own `schemaVersion`, so the envelope cannot bump independently. `report.rs:9,13` adds `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1` and `PACKAGE_REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2`.
  - Type names and numbers diverge: `ReleaseIntentV1` at 5 (`intent.rs:98`), `ReleaseRunEnvelopeV1` at 3 (`execution.rs:93`), `ReleaseReceiptV1` at 2 (`execution.rs:419`). Only `ReleaseDecisionV1` has `READABLE_SCHEMA_VERSIONS` (`decision.rs:120`); the rest require exact match.
  - `callisto-model/src/format/pre.rs:8-13` `PreState { mode, tag, initial_versions, changesets }` has no version field; `callisto-graph/src/lib.rs:324-428` (`assign_promoted_pre_keys`) already migrates legacy bare keys by guessing from git history. `PreState` derives `Deserialize` while `parse_pre_json` hand-parses the same file: two parsers for one format.
  - `release build-python --format json` (`callisto-cli/src/commands/release.rs:286`) prints `{"built": [...]}` with no `schemaVersion`/`command`/`dryRun`; `callisto schema` (`schema.rs:11-22`) publishes 8 types and none of intent, decision, artifact manifest, release-PR files.
  - Fix: envelope gets its own constant with the artifact under a key or `artifactSchemaVersion`; `schemaVersion` on `pre.json` (Changesets ignores unknown keys; absent means 1); route derived `Deserialize` through `parse_pre_json`; route `build-python` through `emit_report`; add schema targets.
  
  **3. Validated wire types expose `pub` fields and unchecked constructors, so the invariant holds only at construction**
  - `callisto-model/src/release/intent.rs:16-24`: `decision`, `snapshot`, `operations`, `artifact_slots` are `pub`, only `digest` is private; `intent.rs:155` hashes `decision.digest.as_str()` without recomputing, so `intent.operations.push(..)` or a struct-literal decision with a forged digest compiles and yields a stale/valid-looking intent. Same shape: `ReleaseDecisionV1`/`ReleaseDecisionEntry` (`decision.rs:45,86`), `ReleaseOperationId` (`operation.rs:217-221`, checked only by `validate_artifact_slot`), `ArtifactSlotId` (`artifact.rs:15-21`).
  - `callisto-model/src/release_pr.rs:653-690`: `ReleasePrCommitPlanV1`'s hand-written `Deserialize` checks only `schema_version`; it never re-runs `validate_commit_plan_path`, `MAX_COMMIT_PLAN_BYTES` or the mode check, so a deserialized plan can contain the `.github/workflows/*` path the doc says it never contains. `ArtifactManifestV1` (`artifact.rs:176-185`) has plain derived `Deserialize`; its gate lives only in `validate_for_intent`.
  - `callisto-model/src/tag.rs:194` `pub fn new_unchecked`, used by `TagTemplate::render` (`tag.rs:93`); `default_for` (`tag.rs:74`) never validates, so build metadata ending `.lock` renders a `TagName` that `TagName::deserialize` rejects.
  - `ReleaseOperationRole::ArtifactUpload { slot }` repeats the id's `package`/`version` (`operation.rs:274-280`) and re-checks equality at runtime (`operation.rs:305-313`).
  - Fix: private fields plus accessors; `TryFrom<Wire>` for commit plan and artifact manifest calling the existing validating constructors; delete `new_unchecked`; drop the duplicated slot fields.
  
  **4. Version grammar is not bound to the package ecosystem by type**
  - `callisto-model/src/version.rs:183-191`: `Version::deserialize` tries SemVer then PEP 440 ("can only guess by trying grammars in turn"); `Serialize` drops the grammar. A PyPI `1.2.3` round-trips as SemVer and `compare` then returns `GrammarMismatch`.
  - Three wire shims each re-implement the fix: `decision.rs:63`, `operation.rs:238`, `artifact.rs:39-40` (`Version::parse_for_ecosystem(&wire.version, wire.package.ecosystem())`), while `ReleaseDecisionV1::new` (`decision.rs:124-180`) and the `ReleaseOperationId` constructors accept any `Version`, so a mismatched grammar is representable in memory and only fails on round trip. `DistributionSetV1.version: String` (`artifact.rs:165`). Plain `Deserialize` is still used by `report.rs:143,148` and `plan.rs`.
  - Fix: one `ReleaseVersion::new(&ReleasePackageId, raw)` as the only entry; remove or gate `Version`'s `Deserialize`.
  
  **5. Package identity is split across `PackageId`, `PackageKey`, `ReleasePackageId` and native names, with silent fallbacks**
  - `callisto-model/src/identity.rs:55-56` `pub enum PackageId { Bare(String), Prefixed {..} }` is publicly constructible without validation; `callisto-graph/src/locate/ignore_walk.rs:173` `PackageId::parse(&name).unwrap_or_else(|_| PackageId::Bare(name.clone()))` builds ids that `PackageId`'s own `Deserialize` (`try_from = "String"`) rejects. `matches` (`identity.rs:164`) is a non-transitive wildcard relation on a type that derives `Eq`/`Hash`/`Ord`.
  - `ReleasePackageId::new` (`callisto-model/src/release/package_id.rs:30`) never canonicalises PyPI names, so `pypi/My_Lib` and `pypi/my-lib` are distinct in `Eq`, digests and tags, while two normalisers exist (`ecosystem.rs:228` joins `_`, `:234` joins `-`). `callisto-graph/src/commands/release_decision.rs:36-38` `.native_name(&package.id, ecosystem).unwrap_or(package.id.name())` silently falls back to the display name.
  - `PackageKey` (ADR 11's "stable identity") has two non-test callers (`commands/matrix.rs:148`, `release/derive.rs:960`) against ~63 `BTreeMap<PackageId, _>` sites keyed by an id that changes on promotion (ADR 11 defers this; it is the revisit trigger).
  - Fix: private `Bare` payload with a validating constructor; PyPI arm in `ReleasePackageId::new`; delete one normaliser; error instead of `unwrap_or`.
  
  **6. Dead and test-only public API shipped from the MIT crate and the libraries**

---

You missed stuff that gets the most out of gix:



<pasted_content id="b815">
Yes, the gix spike may benefit from a cache supplied by Callisto. The object cache Claude tried stores Git objects. It is different from gix’s diff resource cache. In gix 0.90, Tree::changes().for_each_to_obtain_tree() creates a new diff resource cache on every call; gix also provides for_each_to_obtain_tree_with_cache() to reuse one across calls. Its API documentation (https://github.com/GitoxideLabs/gitoxide/blob/main/gix/src/object/tree/diff/for_each.rs) says reuse avoids repeated setup and I/O.

That matters for an 8,002-commit loop. It is especially worth measuring because Callisto needs changed paths with rename tracking disabled: the diff still sets up the resource cache, while the rename processing that would use it is skipped. If the spike used Repository::diff_tree_to_tree(), that is another poor fit for this workload: it creates the cache per call and collects changes into a Vec, whereas Callisto can consume paths as they arrive.

So I would not call the 4.5× gap gix’s best attainable performance yet. The next fair comparison is the same spike with one reused diff resource cache and streamed changes, checking identical paths and timing the history walk again. Reuse could remove repeated setup; it may not close the whole gap because tree traversal still runs for each commit. Without the spike’s source or that measurement, I can identify the missed optimization opportunity, not quantify its benefit.

Callisto’s current Git access (crates/callisto-model/src/vcs/access.rs:510) uses the system binary, so there is no gix cache to add to production code today.
</pasted_content id="b815">

We probably need to dig further into gix's docs and maybe even codebase to optimize our usage further.

---

From codex:



<pasted_content id="b815">
The corrected profile makes the answer narrower. The 640 ms aggregation cost and 220 ms tag matching cost were Callisto algorithms, independent of the Git backend. Your fixes address those; they are not gix defects.

| Operation | What I found |
|---|---|
| Tags and their commits | The old Callisto gix implementation listed tag names, then resolved names separately. Peeling the references during enumeration could avoid repeated revision parsing. Whether the newer spike already does this is unknown. The measured gap is only about 15 ms. |
| Manifest at every tag | gix already won this measurement: about 200 ms versus 255 ms. The revised hash idea’s roughly 25% gain is useful evidence, but there is no demonstrated gix bottleneck here. |
| History with changed paths | This remains the strong candidate. Reusing a diff resource cache, streaming paths, and avoiding unnecessary diff setup are worth testing against the 3.3–3.6 s result. |
| Unmerged tags | The symmetric reachability rule is a Callisto correctness requirement. Any gix comparison must produce the same commit set on both sides of the tag; a one direction tag..HEAD walk would benchmark different behavior. |

Trust observation, staged changes, and signing policy also required system Git in the old two backend design, as recorded in the Git access ADR (docs/adr/0005-system-git-only.md). I do not see another measured Callisto workload that currently justifies a gix performance change. The next useful evidence is a matched history benchmark after the cache and streaming changes, with the merge and hotfix cases included.
</pasted_content id="b815">

I want to lean hard on gix here and really look deep into it. Some context from codex:



<pasted_content id="b815">
This changes my recommendation. The low level gix path is competitive for Callisto’s ordinary history walk. The remaining performance question is much narrower: why repeated reads of a wide, heavily deltified tree spend so much time inflating and copying data.

I would not make gix the default with a several-hundred-MB cache yet. At 4,000 packages, even the measured 1 GB cache leaves gix around 2× slower than Git. A successful but slow gix call will never trigger the proposed error fallback. The 500-package win is 43 ms for the history operation; that is promising, but the rollout decision needs end-to-end status timings and memory use across representative repositories.

There is still Callisto-side tuning to check. In gix 0.90, the pack delta cache and the fully decoded object cache are separate; the latter is unset by default (https://docs.rs/gix/0.90.0/gix/struct.Repository.html#method.object_cache_size_if_unset). I would benchmark both independently, recording peak memory. The small gain from reusing the diff resource cache is good evidence that it is no longer the main lever.

For an upstream contribution, I would start with a reproducible wide-tree history benchmark and pack-resolution profile: cache hit rates, delta-chain depths, bytes inflated and copied, and a cache-size sweep against the same Git-generated pack. Then optimize the measured bottleneck in gix-pack. Gitoxide already treats object caching as workload dependent in its diff performance discussion (https://github.com/GitoxideLabs/gitoxide/discussions/74); its performance backlog (https://github.com/GitoxideLabs/gitoxide/blob/main/etc/plan/bug-fixes.md) also contains pack-related work, though the listed wide-tree pack-size regression is a different issue from this read-time regression.

Claude’s merge approach is plausible for changed path names with rename detection disabled: Git documents that a combined diff lists paths changed from every parent. I would test that equivalence against the exact Git command Callisto uses, including octopus merges and merge resolutions, because --cc also has patch-display rules beyond the path-set rule. Git’s diff-tree documentation (https://git-scm.com/docs/git-diff-tree) is the reference.

For Callisto, I’d implement the read backend as an opt-in pilot, keep the system switch, and gather end-to-end results before choosing a default. Selecting a backend from the first tree’s size is too weak: pack layout, delta depth, and changes later in history drive this regression. The new ADR must also explicitly justify reversing ADR 5 (docs/adr/0005-system-git-only.md), which rejected this same two-backend shape because of semantic drift and dependency cost.
</pasted_content id="b815">


I disagree with the primise of not doing gix until we appropriately setup caches. I think the caches should be relative to the size of the monorepo right?

  They are canonical in code and tests: matrix.rs:498,504 with `canonical_id()`, and matrix_tests.rs:82,87 assert `npm/pkg-a`. The empty-map case printed `schemaVersion: 2` from the binary.   ## (d) docs/architecture/polyglot-v1.md
  The header says "proposed v1 design, not current behavior". That is now wrong for most sections.
  - **Implemented:**
    - The four concepts, with the release owner derived and not stored.
    - Canonical references and migration (the bare/colon rejection is shipped, and the `previous-tag-templates` rename boundary is shipped).
    - Per-edge, per-distribution publish prerequisites.
    - The registry-before-tag-and-announcement default.
    - PyPI file-set completion.
    - Platform packages before their npm owner.
    - The `publish-url` split between index and upload endpoint.
    - The prerelease npm dist-tag.
  - **Still design only:**
    - Co-located Cargo/Maturin version derivation and its refusals. `pep440_rs` is used for parsing only.
    - Embedded/build-input relationships.
    - Authenticated private and HTML-only PyPI, and npm dist-tag promotion.
    - Go and NuGet adapters.
    - Native pack/install tests. The "Prior art" paragraph at line 68 says partial-upload fault injection "remains planned", but #232 shipped it.
  - **Cross-references that need updating:** line 22 ("until the proposed replacement is implemented") and line 39 ("the proposed default").
  - **Stale text elsewhere that points at it:**
    - docs/config.md:5.
    - docs/architecture/identity.md:30 ("Canonical-only new references are planned... not implemented by today's parser").
    - ARCHITECTURE.md:81.
    - docs/specs/INDEX.md:19.
    - docs/specs/REQ-DX-V1.md:5 ("Cargo real-registry tests now exist") and its "Done when" bullets.
    - docs/adr/0003 (line 28 says Python file-set publication is untracked), 0004 (line 25), 0011 (the "Consequences" paragraph links the findings register for "rename, manifest-roster and demotion gaps").
    - ADRs 0001, 0005, 0007, 0009, 0010 and 0011 cite `ROAD-TO-V1.md` "v1 fix plan" sections that no longer exist.
  
  ## (e) ADR 12 (docs/adr/0012)
  Status line is still "Proposed". Its text is accurate against current code.
  - **Conditions met:**
    - Canonical references are required in config, report and matrix keys, and CLI args. The colon form is rejected.
    - Changesets and `pre.json` keep native names (verified in the binary).
    - Ownership is derived and sealed by the intent digest (derive.rs:157-170, walk.rs:282).
    - The default tag is unchanged, and `previous-tag-templates` handles renames.
    - A package's tag waits for every registry publish of the package. A dependent waits only for its own distribution. The product announcement waits for the plugin's tag.
    - Selecting one distribution releases all of them.
    - Reruns observe every required effect (#202, #211, #232, #290).
    - Hooks are left to the package owner.
    - Maturin stays after v1 (E007 reproduced).
  - **Gaps:**
    - The ADR's own opening sentence says it revises ADR 11's selector promotion "when implemented". Whether a partial revision is a supersession is a judgment call for the owner.
    - ADR 12 links to V1-FINDINGS.md and polyglot-v1.md. Both links break or go stale if those files are deleted.
    - I did not check the repo's own `callisto.toml` against the plan's "dogfood callisto.toml and AGENTS.md" step.

    - Looks are 15 s, 45 s, 2 min, then 3 min steps (`policy.rs` `visibility_backoff`).
